BEGIN FILE: docs/future-refactoring.md

# Future refactoring notes

Backlog of structural/maintainability ideas identified during the August 2026
refactor pass. None of these are broken today — they're maintenance and
scaling concerns to revisit as the project grows, not urgent fixes.

## 1. Separate runnable experiment scripts from library code

`src/labtools/acquisition/run_hyperspectral_scan.py` lives inside the
`labtools.acquisition` package alongside genuine reusable library code
(`range_spectrum.py`), but it's really a one-off experiment runner:

- It executes side effects at **module import time**, not just when run as a
  script: `matplotlib.use('Qt5Agg')` and `os.makedirs(OUTPUT_FOLDER, ...)`
  happen unconditionally on `import`, outside the `if __name__ == "__main__":`
  guard. Anything that imports this module for any reason (e.g. to reuse a
  constant) will silently create a directory and switch the global matplotlib
  backend.
- It hardcodes a personal path (`C:\Users\Legend\...`) and one-off experiment
  constants (wavelength range, mirror voltages, laser current, band
  definitions) as module-level globals.
- Its only import of `xarray` is why that dependency was undeclared in
  `pyproject.toml` — it's used by a script, not the core package.

**Suggested fix (later):**
- Move genuinely one-off experiment scripts to a top-level `scripts/` folder
  outside `src/labtools/`, so the installable package stays side-effect-free
  to import.
- Or, at minimum, wrap all module-level side effects (matplotlib backend
  setup, directory creation) inside `main()`/`run()` so importing the module
  never has side effects.
- Centralize hardware addresses/paths/experiment constants (see item 4) so
  scripts don't hardcode a specific user's filesystem layout.

## 2. `spectrum_gui_horiba.py` is still a large single `QMainWindow`

After extracting `AsyncTaskRunner` and the background-math helpers, the
window class (`src/labtools/gui/spectrum_gui_horiba.py`) still owns:
spectrometer connect, laser connect, VISA test, acquisition orchestration,
and save-to-disk, all in one ~570-line class.

**Suggested fix (later):** split into composed panel widgets, e.g. a
`SpectrometerPanel` and a `LaserPanel`, each a `QWidget` embedding its own
controls/signals, composed into the main window. Makes each concern
independently testable and easier to navigate.

## 3. No automated tests beyond the IDQ time controller

`horiba_spectrometer.py`'s frame-combination logic (median/sigma-clip),
`range_spectrum.py`'s dark-frame timing logic, and `gui/background.py`'s
parsing/subtraction are pure-ish logic that could be unit tested with
fakes/mocks for the hardware-dependent bits — no real hardware required.
Currently a regression there would only surface by hand-testing through the
GUI.

**Suggested fix (later):** add unit tests for:
- `HoribaSpectrometer.get_spectrum()`'s median/sigma_clip math (mock
  `_acquire_single`)
- `range_spectrum.py`'s dark-frame timing resolution (`"single"` vs
  `"per_frame"`)
- `gui/background.py`'s `parse_background_csv` / `apply_background_subtraction`
  (already Qt-free, easiest to start with)

## 4. Scattered hardware addresses/config across files

VISA address, Time Controller IP, output paths, laser current, etc. are
hardcoded independently across `itc4000.py`, `run_hyperspectral_scan.py`,
`tests/conftest.py`, and GUI defaults.

**Suggested fix (later):** a single config file (TOML or environment
variables) so changing hardware/IP/paths is a one-line edit instead of a
grep-and-replace across files.

## 5. Minor: `HoribaSpectrometer.PRESETS` is a mutable class-level dict

Flagged by the linter as a mutable default at class scope. Low risk today
since it's only read from, but worth switching to `types.MappingProxyType`
or moving into `__init__` if you want to eliminate the warning and guard
against accidental mutation.

## Known pre-existing gaps (flagged, not fixed)

- `xarray` is imported by `run_hyperspectral_scan.py` but not declared in
  `pyproject.toml` dependencies.
- `README.md` is currently empty — worth a short project overview, `uv`
  setup instructions, and how to launch the GUI.

END FILE: docs/future-refactoring.md

BEGIN FILE: docs/intensity-map-simulation.md
============================================================
# Offline intensity-map simulation, v1

Install on `feature/hardware-simulation`, from a clean committed checkout.
The self-contained installer updates the acquisition and GUI modules in place
using AST-located edits. It adds a simulation package, tests and this guide.
It does not replace the entire GUI or device wrappers.

## Install

    uv run python install_intensity_map_simulation.py --check
    uv run python install_intensity_map_simulation.py
    uv run python -m pytest --noconftest tests/test_intensity_map_simulation.py -q

`--noconftest` isolates this test file from existing hardware fixtures and
collection-time native imports in the repository's conftest files. The tests
provide their own fixtures. Do not add `--run-hardware` to this command.

No additional dependencies are required beyond the project's existing NumPy,
Matplotlib, pytest and (for the GUI) PyQt6. The installer itself uses only the
standard library. It does not run pip, uv sync, or connect devices.

## Launch

    uv run python -m labtools.simulation.intensity_map --gui

This starts the existing intensity-map GUI with simulation selected. It shows
a SIMULATION banner and disables real laser actions. The normal GUI launcher
still defaults to real mode; select the explicit Simulation checkbox to use
synthetic devices. Backend selection is not persisted and cannot change while
a map or laser session is active. Close/reopen the GUI to release a connected
real laser before switching to simulation.

A headless two-channel synthetic map:

    uv run python -m labtools.simulation.intensity_map --points 15

A stopped map:

    uv run python -m labtools.simulation.intensity_map --points 15 --stop-after 40

Resume using the printed dataset directory:

    uv run python -m labtools.simulation.intensity_map --resume "FULL DATASET PATH"

The headless runner defaults to fast operation; `--realtime` honours integration
and settling delays. The GUI uses real-time delays so Stop can be exercised.
The headless --seed option applies to new headless maps; the GUI currently uses
seed 0 and has no seed editor. A resumed map restores its saved seed.

## Model and provenance

The mirror, SPAD gate and counter share a per-session state. Counts come from
a synthetic two-Gaussian scene plus background and Poisson counting noise.
The scene is scaled to the requested voltage range, not to physical distances.
Threshold and edge settings are recorded/validated but do not model detector
electronics. Integration time scales expected counts. Closed gate gives zero.
The per-coordinate/channel seed makes resumed counts match uninterrupted maps.
This is synthetic development data, not a model validated against your optics.

New synthetic datasets go under an additional SIMULATION subdirectory and have
SYNTHETIC_DATA.txt plus explicit JSON and final NPZ provenance. PNG titles and
live plot titles are marked SYNTHETIC. Existing CSV names/schema are retained:
keep them with the dataset metadata and marker, not as standalone measurements.
Real checkpoint signatures retain their legacy shape. Cross-backend resume is
rejected before device creation. A simulation never silently replaces a failed
real connection. Simulation seed/model changes invalidate simulation resume;
real-time delay and fault-injection changes do not.

Failure injection is available programmatically:

    config = dataclasses.replace(config, simulation_fail_after_points=4)

Clear this option before resuming. It counts measurements in the current session.

## Limits and validation

This first phase covers intensity mapping only. It does not simulate the camera,
annealing, shutter, rotation stage, spectrometer or laser power. Real laser
controls are disabled rather than simulated. Existing native-driver issues and
physical safety checks are not solved by simulation.

The package includes offline tests for one/two-channel maps, stop/resume,
fault checkpoints, seed reproducibility, provenance, backend isolation and GUI
laser-action guards. These do not prove Qt rendering, real-device behaviour,
physical timing, wiring or calibration. Run the tests on your actual checkout.

The development validation used a representative acquisition fixture rebuilt
from the retrieved source interfaces, not a byte-for-byte local copy of the
current repository. Full Qt/hardware execution was unavailable.

## Rollback

    uv run python install_intensity_map_simulation.py --rollback

Backups are in .labtools-migrations/intensity-map-simulation-v1. Do not commit
that directory. Rollback refuses to overwrite files modified after installation,
including formatting changes. Review the diff and keep the backup until tested.

END FILE: docs/intensity-map-simulation.md

BEGIN FILE: docs/operator-workspace.md
============================================================
# Operator workspace (annealing first phase)

The installer adds recipes/profile validation and GUI load/save controls for laser annealing.
Intensity mapping receives a launcher only; its recipe migration is not implemented here.
Camera preferences and existing QSettings persistence are retained. Recipes exclude hardware
connections, maintainer limits, camera preferences and safety acknowledgements.

## Maintainer setup

Review examples/profiles/instrument.example.json. All example limits and coordinates are
unverified starting values, NOT a power calibration. Set real instrument limits and a unique
profile_id, then set approved to true only after review. Install the profile at
C:/ProgramData/Labtools/instrument.json and use Windows permissions to allow operators read
access but no modification. Do not use a user-writable profile for a protected deployment.
LABTOOLS_INSTRUMENT_PROFILE overrides the path for development/testing; it is NOT a security
boundary. An operator able to execute arbitrary Python can bypass these software guardrails.
Do not treat the approved flag as independent evidence of calibration or safety.

Run uv sync after installation to refresh entry points. The installer does not modify uv.lock;
review any lock changes produced by uv sync. Do not install development code editable in the
protected deployment. Build/install a tested wheel with a separately created environment and
machine-accessible Python. Keep vendor drivers/DLLs available to each account. Windows ACL
setup and production deployment are deliberately NOT performed by this installer.

## Operator use

labtools init "C:/Users/OPERATOR/Documents/Labtools Experiments"
labtools doctor
labtools validate "C:/Users/OPERATOR/Documents/Labtools Experiments/recipes/anneal.json"
labtools-anneal

Use Load recipe / Save recipe / Save recipe as / Restore recipe defaults in the GUI.
Profile hardware fields override GUI hardware preferences. Requested maximum open time may
be reduced but cannot exceed the profile. Recipe load clears the optical-path acknowledgement.
Load/save/reset is refused during an anneal. Recipe loading does not connect or move hardware.
Manual stage moves are checked against profile coordinates before confirmation.

The CLI anneal command operates hardware only with --confirm-optical-path. Physical checks
and interlocks remain essential. Editable runner examples default to validation only.

## Metadata and limitations

Existing metadata.json includes resolved dataclass settings plus instrument_profile_id,
instrument_profile_sha256 and software_version. The profile digest identifies the resolved
profile, not a digital signature. The existing maximum-open-time check is an estimate based on dwell and settle, not an
independent hard shutter deadline. Native driver calls can block. Hardware interlocks remain
required. This update does not change acquisition timing or device shutdown.

This update preserves existing camera and serial shutdown code; it does not fix driver hangs.
The existing QSettings values are per-user convenience settings, not named recipes.
Only maintainers should change the installed core; operators edit their workspace.

## Existing scripts

Direct calls to run_laser_anneal(LaserAnnealConfig(...)) now require resolved profile metadata.
Migrate them to build_config(recipe, profile); the GUI is patched to resolve the profile.
Existing hardware integration tests may need corresponding fixtures. The profile check runs
before the public anneal function opens devices. Internal helpers and direct device wrappers
are not a security boundary. The installer does not modify those wrappers.

Backups live in .labtools-migrations; do not commit that folder. Rollback checks hashes and
refuses to overwrite files changed after installation, including by a formatter.

END FILE: docs/operator-workspace.md

BEGIN FILE: examples/experiments/run_anneal_recipe.py
============================================================
"""Editable runner. Defaults to validation; --run operates hardware."""
import argparse
from pathlib import Path
from labtools.config.anneal_workspace import load_profile, load_recipe, validate_recipe


def main():
    parser = argparse.ArgumentParser()
    parser.add_argument("recipe", type=Path)
    parser.add_argument("--run", action="store_true")
    args = parser.parse_args()
    profile = load_profile()
    recipe = load_recipe(args.recipe)
    validate_recipe(recipe, profile)
    print("Recipe valid")
    if args.run:
        answer = input("Optical path checked and ready to expose sample? Type RUN: ")
        if answer != "RUN":
            raise SystemExit("Cancelled")
        from labtools.config.anneal_workspace import build_config
        from labtools.acquisition.laser_anneal import run_laser_anneal
        run_laser_anneal(build_config(recipe, profile), status_callback=print)


if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()

END FILE: examples/experiments/run_anneal_recipe.py

No devices are opened. Apply on feature/hardware-simulation.
Backups and guarded --rollback are provided. Python 3.12+.
"""

config\n\n    def move(self, x_voltage, y_voltage):\n        values = (float(x_voltage), float(y_voltage))\n        if self.state.closed:\n            raise RuntimeError(\'Simulated session is (c.y_start_v + c.y_stop_v) / 2) / sy\n        scene = 2000.0 + 80000.0 * math.exp(-0.5 * ((u / .17) ** 2 + (v / .20) ** 2))\n        scene += 18000.0 * math.exp(-0.5 * (((u - .27) / .09) ** 2 + ((v + .22) / .12) ** 2))\n        result = {}\n        for channel in channels:\n            # Per-position/channel RNG makes resumed maps match uninterrupted maps.\n            key = f\'{MODEL_VERSION}|{c.simulation_seed}|{self.state.x_v.hex()}|{self.state.y_v.hex()}|{channel}|{integration_time_s.hex()}\'\n            seed = int.from_bytes(hashlib.sha256(key.encode()).digest()[:16], \'little\')\n            rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)\n            rate = scene * (1.0 + .25 * (channel - 1)) if self.state.gate_open else 0.0\n            result[channel] = int(rng.poisson(rate * integration_time_s))\n        self.state.measurements += 1\n        return result\n\n\n@contextmanager\ndef open_map_devices(config):\n signature\n', 'docs/intensity-map-simulation.md': '# Offline intensity-map simulation, v1\n\nInstall on `feature/hardware-simulation`, from a clean committed checkout.\nThe self-contained installer updates the acquisition and GUI modules in place\nusing AST-located edits. It adds a simulation package, tests and this guide.\nIt does not replace the entire GUI or device wrappers.\n\n## Install\n\n    uv run python install_intensity_map_simulation.py --check\n    uv run python install_intensity_map_simulation.py\n    uv run python -m pytest --noconftest tests/test_intensity_map_simulation.py -q\n\n`--noconftest` isolates this test file from existing hardware fixtures and\ncollection-time native imports in the repository\'s conftest files. The tests\nprovide their own fixtures. Do not add `--run-hardware` to this command.\n\nNo additional dependencies are required beyond the project\'s existing NumPy,\nMatplotlib, pytest and (for the GUI) PyQt6. The installer itself uses only the\nstandard library. It does not run pip, uv sync, or connect devices.\n\n## Launch\n\n    uv run python -m labtools.simulation.intensity_map --gui\n\nThis starts the existing intensity-map GUI with simulation selected. It shows\na SIMULATION banner and disables real laser actions. The normal GUI launcher\nstill defaults to real mode; select the explicit Simulation checkbox to use\nsynthetic devices. Backend selection is not persisted and cannot change while\na map or laser session is active. Close/reopen the GUI to release a connected\nreal laser before switching to simulation.\n\nA headless two-channel synthetic map:\n\n    uv run python -m labtools.simulation.intensity_map --points 15\n\nA stopped map:\n\n    uv run python -m labtools.simulation.intensity_map --points 15 --stop-after 40\n\nResume using the printed dataset directory:\n\n    uv run python -m labtools.simulation.intensity_map --resume "FULL DATASET PATH"\n\nThe headless runner defaults to fast operation; `--realtime` honours integration\nand settling delays. The GUI uses real-time delays so Stop can be exercised.\nThe headless --seed option applies to new headless maps; the GUI currently uses\nseed 0 and has no seed editor. A resumed map restores its saved seed.\n\n## Model and provenance\n\nThe mirror, SPAD gate and counter share a per-session state. Counts come from\na synthetic two-Gaussian scene plus background and Poisson counting noise.\nThe scene is scaled to the requested voltage range, not to physical distances.\nThreshold and edge settings are recorded/validated but do not model detector\nelectronics. Integration time scales expected counts. Closed gate gives zero.\nThe per-coordinate/channel seed makes resumed counts match uninterrupted maps.\nThis is synthetic development data, not a model validated against your optics.\n\nNew synthetic datasets go under an additional SIMULATION subdirectory and have\nSYNTHETIC_DATA.txt plus explicit JSON and final NPZ provenance. PNG titles and\nlive plot titles are marked SYNTHETIC. Existing CSV names/schema are retained:\nkeep them with the dataset metadata and marker, not as standalone measurements.\nReal checkpoint signatures retain their legacy shape. Cross-backend resume is\nrejected before device creation. A simulation never silently replaces a failed\nreal connection. Simulation seed/model changes invalidate simulation resume;\nreal-time delay and fault-injection changes do not.\n\nFailure injection is available programmatically:\n\n    config = dataclasses.replace(config, simulation_fail_after_points=4)\n\nClear this option before resuming. It counts measurements in the current session.\n\n## Limits and validation\n\nThis first phase covers intensity mapping only. It does not simulate the camera,\nannealing, shutter, rotation stage, spectrometer or laser power. Real laser\ncontrols are disabled rather than simulated. Existing native-driver issues and\nphysical safety checks are not solved by simulation.\n\nThe package includes offline tests for one/two-channel maps, stop/resume,\nfault checkpoints, seed reproducibility, provenance, backend isolation and GUI\nlaser-action guards. These do not prove Qt rendering, real-device behaviour,\nphysical timing, wiring or calibration. Run the tests on your actual checkout.\n\nThe development validation used a representative acquisition fixture rebuilt\nfrom the retrieved source interfaces, not a byte-for-byte local copy of the\ncurrent repository. Full Qt/hardware execution was unavailable.\n\n## Rollback\n\n    uv run python install_intensity_map_simulation.py --rollback\n\nBackups are in .labtools-migrations/intensity-map-simulation-v1. Do not commit\nthat directory. Rollback refuses to overwrite files modified after installation,\nincluding formatting changes. Review the diff and keep the backup until tested.\n'}

"""AST-located source edits, refusing unknown layouts rather than guessing."""
import ast


def one(nodes, name):
    matches = [n for n in nodes if getattr(n, 'name', None) == name]
    if len(matches) != 1:
        raise ValueError(f'Expected one {name}; found {len(matches)}. No changes written.')
    return matches[0]


def apply(source, edits):
    lines = source.splitlines(keepends=True)
    for start, end, replacement in sorted(edits, reverse=True):
        lines[start:end] = [replacement]
    result = ''.join(lines)
    compile(result, '<proposed update>', 'exec')
    return result


def body_start(node):
    first = node.body[0]
    if isinstance(first, ast.Expr) and isinstance(first.value, ast.Constant) and isinstance(first.value.value, str):
        return first.end_lineno
    return first.lineno - 1


def patch_acquisition(source):
    tree = ast.parse(source)
    cls = one(tree.body, 'IntensityMapConfig')
    if any(isinstance(n, ast.AnnAssign) and getattr(n.target, 'id', '') == 'backend' for n in cls.body):
        raise ValueError('A backend already exists; refusing duplicate migration')
    acquire = one(tree.body, 'acquire_intensity_map')
    edits = []
    modules = {'labtools.devices.idq_time_controller', 'labtools.devices.labjack_u6',
               'labtools.devices.scanning_mirror', 'labtools.devices.spad_gate'}
    imports = [n for n in tree.body if isinstance(n, ast.ImportFrom) and n.module in modules]
    if len(imports) != 4:
        raise ValueError('Expected four device imports in acquisition module')
    for n in imports:
        edits.append((n.lineno - 1, n.end_lineno, ''))
    first = min(n.lineno for n in imports) - 1
    edits.append((first, first, 'from labtools.simulation.map_backend import MODEL_VERSION, open_map_devices\n'))
    fields = [n for n in cls.body if isinstance(n, ast.AnnAssign)]
    edits.append((fields[-1].end_lineno, fields[-1].end_lineno, '''    backend: str = "real"
    simulation_seed: int = 0
    simulation_realtime: bool = True
    simulation_fail_after_points: int | None = None
'''))
    validate = one(cls.body, 'validate')
    edits.append((body_start(validate), body_start(validate), '''        if self.backend not in {"real", "simulation"}:
            raise ValueError("backend must be real or simulation")
        if type(self.simulation_seed) is not int or self.simulation_seed < 0:
            raise ValueError("simulation_seed must be a nonnegative integer")
        if type(self.simulation_realtime) is not bool:
            raise ValueError("simulation_realtime must be bool")
        if self.simulation_fail_after_points is not None and (
            type(self.simulation_fail_after_points) is not int or self.simulation_fail_after_points < 0
        ):
            raise ValueError("simulation_fail_after_points must be nonnegative or None")
        if not all(np.isfinite(v) for v in (
            self.integration_time_s, self.settle_time_s,
            self.safe_voltage_min_v, self.safe_voltage_max_v,
        )):
            raise ValueError("Timing and voltage limits must be finite")
'''))
    signature = one(cls.body, 'checkpoint_signature')
    ret = [n for n in signature.body if isinstance(n, ast.Return)]
    if len(ret) != 1 or not isinstance(ret[0].value, ast.Dict):
        raise ValueError('Unexpected checkpoint signature implementation')
    node = ret[0]
    text = ast.unparse(node.value)
    replacement = '        signature = ' + text + '\n'
    replacement += '''        if self.backend == "simulation":
            signature.update(backend="simulation", simulation_seed=self.simulation_seed,
                             simulation_model=MODEL_VERSION)
        return signature
'''
    edits.append((node.lineno - 1, node.end_lineno, replacement))
    # Preserve legacy real checkpoint signatures; explicitly forbid cross-backend resume.
    resume = one(tree.body, '_validate_resume_config')
    edits.append((body_start(resume), body_start(resume), '''    if metadata.get("backend", "real") != config.backend:
        raise ValueError("Cannot resume data with a different real/simulation backend")
'''))
    metadata = one(tree.body, '_write_metadata')
    payload = [n for n in metadata.body if isinstance(n, ast.Assign) and any(ast.unparse(t) == 'payload' for t in n.targets)]
    if len(payload) != 1:
        raise ValueError('Cannot identify metadata payload')
    at = payload[0].end_lineno
    edits.append((at, at, '''    payload.update(synthetic_data=config.backend == "simulation",
                   simulation_model=MODEL_VERSION if config.backend == "simulation" else None)
'''))
    # Replace only the hardware setup header; preserve the existing scan body.
    contexts = [n for n in ast.walk(acquire) if isinstance(n, ast.With) and
                any('LabJackU6' in ast.unparse(item.context_expr) for item in n.items)]
    if len(contexts) != 1:
        raise ValueError('Cannot identify hardware setup context')
    context = contexts[0]
    if len(context.body) < 3 or not isinstance(context.body[0], ast.Assign) or not isinstance(context.body[1], ast.Assign):
        raise ValueError('Unexpected mirror/gate setup layout')
    if ast.unparse(context.body[0].targets[0]) != 'mirror' or ast.unparse(context.body[1].targets[0]) != 'gate':
        raise ValueError('Expected mirror and gate setup before channel configuration')
    indent = ' ' * context.col_offset
    edits.append((context.lineno - 1, context.body[1].end_lineno,
                  indent + 'with open_map_devices(config) as (mirror, gate, controller):\n'))
    # Separate output roots and add a plain-language marker before any data are saved.
    assignments = [n for n in ast.walk(acquire) if isinstance(n, ast.Assign) and
                   isinstance(n.value, ast.Call) and ast.unparse(n.value.func) == '_new_output_directory']
    if len(assignments) != 1:
        raise ValueError('Cannot identify output directory creation')
    n = assignments[0]
    edits.append((n.lineno - 1, n.end_lineno, '''        root = Path(config.output_root)
        if config.backend == "simulation":
            root = root / "SIMULATION"
        if config.backend == "simulation":
            output_directory = root / datetime.now(UTC).strftime("synthetic_%Y%m%d-%H%M%S-%f")
            output_directory.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=False)
        else:
            output_directory = _new_output_directory(root)
        if config.backend == "simulation":
            (output_directory / "SYNTHETIC_DATA.txt").write_text(
                "SIMULATION: synthetic development data, not experimental measurements.\\n",
                encoding="utf-8",
            )
'''))
    settle = [n for n in ast.walk(acquire) if isinstance(n, ast.If) and ast.unparse(n.test) == 'config.settle_time_s']
    if len(settle) != 1:
        raise ValueError('Cannot identify settling condition')
    n = settle[0]
    edits.append((n.lineno - 1, n.lineno, ' ' * n.col_offset +
                  'if config.settle_time_s and (config.backend == "real" or config.simulation_realtime):\n'))
    final = one(tree.body, '_save_final_outputs')
    # Add provenance directly inside the final NPZ, and label the PNG.
    calls = [n for n in ast.walk(final) if isinstance(n, ast.Call) and ast.unparse(n.func) == 'np.savez_compressed']
    if len(calls) != 1:
        raise ValueError('Cannot identify final NPZ write')
    n = calls[0]
    edits.append((n.end_lineno - 1, n.end_lineno - 1, '''        backend=config.backend,
        synthetic_data=config.backend == "simulation",
'''))
    title = [n for n in ast.walk(final) if isinstance(n, ast.Call) and ast.unparse(n.func) == 'plot_intensity_map']
    if len(title) != 1:
        raise ValueError('Cannot identify final plot')
    call = title[0]
    title_kw = next(k for k in call.keywords if k.arg == 'title')
    if title_kw.value.lineno != title_kw.value.end_lineno:
        raise ValueError('Unexpected plot title layout')
    edits.append((title_kw.value.lineno - 1, title_kw.value.end_lineno,
                  '        title=("SYNTHETIC — " if config.backend == "simulation" else "") + _plot_title(result.channels),\n'))
    return apply(source, edits)


GUI_METHODS = '''
    def _simulation_changed(self, enabled):
        """Choose backend explicitly, never while a session owns hardware."""
        if self._thread is not None or self.laser is not None:
            previous = self.simulation_mode.blockSignals(True)
            self.simulation_mode.setChecked(not enabled)
            self.simulation_mode.blockSignals(previous)
            QMessageBox.warning(self, "Backend locked", "Stop the map and disconnect the laser before switching backend.")
            return
        if enabled:
            self._real_output_root = self.output_root.text()
            self.output_root.setText(str(Path.home() / "Labtools Simulation"))
        elif hasattr(self, "_real_output_root"):
            self.output_root.setText(self._real_output_root)
        self.simulation_banner.setVisible(enabled)
        self.setWindowTitle("SIMULATION — Intensity Map" if enabled else "Intensity Map Acquisition")
        self.laser_group.setEnabled(not enabled)
        self.set_laser_indicator(False)
        self.set_status("SIMULATION — synthetic data only" if enabled else "Real hardware selected")

'''


def patch_gui(source):
    tree = ast.parse(source)
    cls = one(tree.body, 'IntensityMapWindow')
    if 'simulation_mode' in source:
        raise ValueError('Simulation GUI controls already exist')
    edits = []
    imports = [n for n in tree.body if isinstance(n, ast.ImportFrom) and n.module == 'labtools.devices.itc4000']
    if len(imports) != 1:
        raise ValueError('Expected ITC4000 import')
    n = imports[0]
    edits.append((n.lineno - 1, n.end_lineno,
                  'from labtools.simulation.map_backend import LazyITC4000 as ITC4000\n'))
    init = one(cls.body, '__init__')
    calls = [n for n in init.body if isinstance(n, ast.Expr) and isinstance(n.value, ast.Call)
             and ast.unparse(n.value.func) == 'self._build_map_controls']
    if len(calls) != 1:
        raise ValueError('Cannot identify map controls construction')
    n = calls[0]
    edits.append((n.end_lineno, n.end_lineno, '''        self.simulation_mode = QCheckBox("Simulation — no hardware")
        self.simulation_mode.setChecked(False)
        controls_layout.insertWidget(0, self.simulation_mode)
'''))
    edits.append((init.end_lineno, init.end_lineno, '''        self.simulation_banner = QLabel("SIMULATION — SYNTHETIC DATA ONLY")
        self.simulation_banner.setStyleSheet("background:#fff3cd;color:#664d03;padding:6px;font-weight:bold;")
        self.statusBar().addPermanentWidget(self.simulation_banner)
        self.simulation_banner.hide()
        self.simulation_mode.toggled.connect(self._simulation_changed)
'''))
    config = one(cls.body, '_config')
    ret = next(n for n in config.body if isinstance(n, ast.Return))
    if not isinstance(ret.value, ast.Call):
        raise ValueError('Unexpected GUI config builder')
    edits.append((ret.value.end_lineno - 1, ret.value.end_lineno - 1,
                  '            backend="simulation" if self.simulation_mode.isChecked() else "real",\n'))
    start = one(cls.body, '_start_worker')
    edits.append((body_start(start), body_start(start), '''        selected = "simulation" if self.simulation_mode.isChecked() else "real"
        if config.backend != selected:
            QMessageBox.warning(self, "Backend mismatch", "Select the matching backend before resuming this map.")
            return
'''))
    thread = [n for n in start.body if isinstance(n, ast.Assign) and any(ast.unparse(t) == 'self._thread' for t in n.targets)]
    if len(thread) != 1:
        raise ValueError('Cannot identify worker thread creation')
    edits.append((thread[0].lineno - 1, thread[0].lineno - 1,
                  '        self.simulation_mode.setEnabled(False)\n'))
    cleanup = one(cls.body, '_cleanup_worker')
    edits.append((body_start(cleanup), body_start(cleanup), '        self.simulation_mode.setEnabled(True)\n'))
    resume = one(cls.body, 'choose_resume')
    apply_calls = [n for n in resume.body if isinstance(n, ast.Expr) and isinstance(n.value, ast.Call)
                   and ast.unparse(n.value.func) == 'self._apply_config']
    if len(apply_calls) != 1:
        raise ValueError('Cannot identify resume config application')
    at = apply_calls[0].lineno - 1
    edits.append((at, at, '''        selected = "simulation" if self.simulation_mode.isChecked() else "real"
        if config.backend != selected:
            QMessageBox.warning(self, "Backend mismatch", "Select the matching real/simulation backend before choosing this checkpoint.")
            self._resume_directory = None
            return
'''))
    for name in ('connect_laser', 'turn_laser_on', 'turn_laser_off', 'set_laser_current'):
        method = one(cls.body, name)
        at = body_start(method)
        edits.append((at, at, '''        if self.simulation_mode.isChecked():
            self.set_status("SIMULATION — real laser controls are disabled")
            return
'''))
    toggle = one(cls.body, '_toggle_laser_controls')
    at = body_start(toggle)
    edits.append((at, at, '''        if getattr(self, "simulation_mode", None) is not None and self.simulation_mode.isChecked():
            self.laser_group.setEnabled(False)
            return
'''))
    status = one(cls.body, 'set_status')
    at = body_start(status)
    edits.append((at, at, '        if getattr(self, "simulation_mode", None) is not None and self.simulation_mode.isChecked():\n            text = "SIMULATION — " + text\n'))
    progress = one(cls.body, '_on_progress')
    titles = [n for n in progress.body if isinstance(n, ast.Assign) and any(ast.unparse(t) == 'title' for t in n.targets)]
    if len(titles) != 1:
        raise ValueError('Cannot identify progress title')
    at = titles[0].end_lineno
    edits.append((at, at, '        if self.simulation_mode.isChecked():\n            title = "SYNTHETIC — " + title\n'))
    edits.append((cls.end_lineno, cls.end_lineno, GUI_METHODS))
    return apply(source, edits)


ACQUISITION = Path('src/labtools/acquisition/intensity_map.py')
GUI = Path('src/labtools/gui/intensity_map_gui.py')
STATE = Path('.labtools-migrations/intensity-map-simulation-v1')


def digest(data):
    return hashlib.sha256(data).hexdigest()


def laser_address():
    """Read the existing literal without importing its VISA dependencies."""
    path = Path('src/labtools/devices/itc4000.py')
    tree = ast.parse(path.read_text(encoding='utf-8-sig'))
    cls = one(tree.body, 'ITC4000')
    for node in cls.body:
        if isinstance(node, ast.Assign) and any(getattr(t, 'id', '') == 'DEFAULT_ADDRESS' for t in node.targets):
            value = ast.literal_eval(node.value)
            if isinstance(value, str):
                return value
        if isinstance(node, ast.AnnAssign) and getattr(node.target, 'id', '') == 'DEFAULT_ADDRESS':
            value = ast.literal_eval(node.value)
            if isinstance(value, str):
                return value
    raise ValueError('Cannot read existing ITC4000 DEFAULT_ADDRESS; nothing changed')


def plan():
    if not Path('pyproject.toml').is_file():
        raise ValueError('Run from the repository root containing pyproject.toml')
    changes = dict(PAYLOAD)
    for name in changes:
        if Path(name).exists():
            raise ValueError(f'New target already exists; refusing to overwrite: {name}')
    changes[str(ACQUISITION)] = patch_acquisition(ACQUISITION.read_text(encoding='utf-8-sig'))
    changes[str(GUI)] = patch_gui(GUI.read_text(encoding='utf-8-sig'))
    backend_path = 'src/labtools/simulation/map_backend.py'
    changes[backend_path] = changes[backend_path].replace(
        "'__INSTALLER_LASER_ADDRESS__'", repr(laser_address())
    )
    for name, text in changes.items():
        if name.endswith('.py'):
            compile(text, name, 'exec')
    # Static guard: native imports must not remain at module scope.
    for name in (str(ACQUISITION), str(GUI)):
        for node in ast.parse(changes[name]).body:
            if isinstance(node, ast.ImportFrom) and (node.module or '').startswith('labtools.devices'):
                raise ValueError(f'Native device import remains at module scope: {name}')
    return {name: text.encode('utf-8') for name, text in changes.items()}


def rollback():
    manifest = STATE / 'manifest.json'
    records = json.loads(manifest.read_text(encoding='utf-8'))
    for name, record in records.items():
        path = Path(name)
        if not path.is_file() or digest(path.read_bytes()) != record['installed']:
            raise ValueError(f'Refusing to overwrite changed file: {name}')
    for name, record in records.items():
        path = Path(name)
        if record['original'] is None:
            path.unlink()
        else:
            path.write_bytes(bytes.fromhex(record['original']))
    manifest.unlink()
    print('[OK] Original files restored; no hardware opened')


def main():
    parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(description=__doc__)
    group = parser.add_mutually_exclusive_group()
    group.add_argument('--check', action='store_true')
    group.add_argument('--rollback', action='store_true')
    args = parser.parse_args()
    if args.rollback:
        rollback()
        return
    manifest = STATE / 'manifest.json'
    if manifest.exists():
        raise ValueError('Simulation update already installed; do not apply twice')
    changes = plan()
    for name in changes:
        print('Update:', name)
    if args.check:
        print('[OK] Proposed Python files compile. No files changed or devices opened.')
        return
    originals = {name: Path(name).read_bytes() if Path(name).exists() else None for name in changes}
    records = {name: {'original': data.hex() if data is not None else None,
                      'installed': digest(changes[name])} for name, data in originals.items()}
    STATE.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
    manifest.write_text(json.dumps(records, indent=2), encoding='utf-8')
    try:
        for name, data in changes.items():
            path = Path(name)
            path.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
            path.write_bytes(data)
    except BaseException:
        for name, data in originals.items():
            if data is None:
                Path(name).unlink(missing_ok=True)
            else:
                Path(name).write_bytes(data)
        manifest.unlink()
        raise
    print('[OK] Installed; no devices opened.')
    print('Backups:', STATE)
    print('Do not commit .labtools-migrations.')
    print('Test: uv run python -m pytest --noconftest tests/test_intensity_map_simulation.py -q')
    print('GUI: uv run python -m labtools.simulation.intensity_map --gui')


if __name__ == '__main__':
    main()

END FILE: install_intensity_map_simulation.py

# labtools

Python tools for laboratory automation, instrument control, spectrum acquisition, data processing and experimental diagnostics.

The package currently focuses on:

- HORIBA iHR spectrometer and CCD control
- stitched wavelength-range acquisition
- Syncerity and Symphony detector selection
- open-shutter and dark-frame acquisition
- repeated-frame averaging and outlier rejection
- reusable dark spectra
- ITC4000 laser-controller operation
- PyQt6 acquisition interfaces
- reproducible hardware diagnostics

## Project status

The HORIBA range-acquisition workflow is operational, including:

- CCD and monochromator discovery
- wavelength-axis calibration
- stitched range acquisition
- Slit A control
- grating-position readback
- repeated-frame combination
- acquisition-time dark subtraction modes
- reusable stitched dark spectra
- USB connection checks
- graphical spectrum display

The physical shutter is controlled by detector TTL outputs through an SDrive-500 shutter controller.

Background-subtraction behaviour should be validated after any change to detector wiring, shutter configuration or SDrive operation.

## Repository structure

```text
labtools/
├── docs/
├── scripts/
│   └── horiba/
│       ├── check_horiba_configuration.py
│       ├── set_horiba_configuration.py
│       ├── check_horiba_shutter.py
│       └── check_horiba_slit_width.py
├── src/
│   └── labtools/
│       ├── acquisition/
│       │   └── range_spectrum.py
│       ├── devices/
│       │   ├── horiba_spectrometer.py
│       │   └── itc4000.py
│       ├── gui/
│       │   ├── async_runner.py
│       │   └── spectrum_gui_horiba.py
│       ├── io/
│       ├── processing/
│       └── visualisation/
├── tests/
├── vendor/
├── pyproject.toml
├── uv.lock
└── README.md
```

The exact contents may change as additional instruments and workflows are added.

## Requirements

- Windows 10 or Windows 11
- Python version defined in `.python-version`
- [`tps://docs.astral.sh/uv/
- HORIBA EzSpec SDK and ICL
- the appropriate HORIBA firmware and spectrometer configuration
- PyQt6
- NumPy
- Matplotlib
- Loguru
- PyVISA for supported VISA instruments

The HORIBA SDK and instrument configuration are system-specific and may require vendor software outside this repository.

## Installation

Clone the repository:

```powershell
cd labtools
```

Create or synchronise the environment:

```powershell

```

If development dependencies are defined:

```powershell
uv sync --dev
```

Check that the package imports correctly:

```powershell
uv run python -c "import labtools; print('labtools import successful')"
```

## Code-quality checks

Compile the main HORIBA modules:

```powershell
uv run python -m py_compile `
    .\src\labtools\devices\horiba_spectrometer.py `
    .\src\labtools\acquisition\range_spectrum.py `
    .\src\labtools\gui\spectrum_gui_horiba.py
```

Format the code:

```powershell

```

Run lint checks:

```powershell

```

Run the test suite:

```powershell

```

Some integration tests or diagnostic scripts require connected laboratory hardware and should not be treated as ordinary offline unit tests.

## HORIBA spectrum-acquisition GUI

Launch the GUI from the repository root:

```powershell
uv run python `
    .\src\labtools\gui\spectrum_gui_horiba.py
```

The GUI provides controls for:

- detector preset
- start and end wavelength
- exposure time
- number of repeated frames
- frame-combination method
- stitching overlap
- Slit A width
- dark-subtraction mode
- reusable dark capture and loading
- optional CSV and PNG output
- optional ITC4000 control

The GUI presently opens, configures, uses and closes the HORIBA connection for each operation. This keeps the HORIBA WebSocket on the same asyncio event loop throughout an acquisition.

A future improvement is to use one persistent worker thread and asyncio event loop so multiple spectra can be acquired without reconnecting between operations.

## Detector presets

The wrapper currently maps detector presets to CCD discovery indices:

```python
_PRESET_TO_CCD_INDEX = {
    "symphony": 0,
    "syncerity": 1,
}
```

These mappings depend on the device order returned by the installed HORIBA configuration.

The preset does not force a grating position.

The software reads and reports the current grating turret position because the assignment of physical gratings to `FIRST`, `SECOND` and `THIRD` is defined by the spectrometer firmware configuration.

If device discovery order changes, verify that each preset still selects the intended detector.

## HORIBA acquisition configuration

The CCD is configured for one-dimensional spectral acquisition using:

- `AcquisitionFormat.SPECTRA_IMAGE`
- one acquisition per request
- full-height vertical binning by default
- exposure time specified in seconds at the `labtools` API
- millisecond timer resolution in the HORIBA SDK
- configurable gain and speed tokens

For the current Syncerity configuration:

```text
Gain token 0: High Light
Gain token 1: Best Dynamic Range
Gain token 2: High Sensitivity

Speed token 0:   45 kHz
Speed token 1:   1 MHz
Speed token 2:   1 MHz Ultra
Speed token 127: 500 kHz Wrap
```

These token meanings come from the connected detector configuration and should not be assumed to apply to every HORIBA camera.

## Wavelength-range acquisition

Range acquisition is implemented in:

```text
src/labtools/acquisition/range_spectrum.py
```

The overall sequence is:

```text
connect to the monochromator and CCD
configure the CCD
prepare the calibrated wavelength axis
calculate required monochromator centre wavelengths
move to each centre wavelength
acquire the detector spectrum
stitch the spectra
trim the stitched result to the requested interval
disconnect cleanly
```

The monochromator centre wavelength shown in the log is the centre used for a detector capture. It is not necessarily the minimum, maximum or midpoint of the final trimmed output range.

## Frame-combination modes

The HORIBA wrapper supports:

```text
single
mean
median
sigma_clip
```

### `single`

Returns one detector frame.

### `mean`

Calculates the arithmetic mean of repeated frames.

### `median`

Calculates the pixel-wise median of repeated frames.

### `sigma_clip`

Uses a robust median and median absolute deviation estimate to reject strong pixel-level outliers before averaging.

At least three frames are required for `sigma_clip`.

## Slit control

The GUI exposes only:

```text
Slit A width
```

The requested width is specified in millimetres.

The wrapper:

1. sends the movement request;
2. allows ICL to register the movement;
3. waits for the monochromator;
4. reads the reported Slit A position;
5. logs the requested and reported values.

Use only widths supported by the installed monochromator, firmware and experimental configuration.

## Shutter-control architecture

The iHR320 electromechanical shutter is driven by an SDrive-500 shutter controller.

The present hardware arrangement uses:

```text
Syncerity TTL output ─┐
                      ├─> SDrive-500 trigger inputs
Symphony TTL output ──┘

SDrive-500 shutter output ──> iHR320 electromechanical shutter
```

The SDrive-500 has two active-high TTL inputs.

The shutter is open whenever either trigger input is high.

This has an important consequence:

> An inactive detector can hold the shared shutter open if its TTL output remains high.

During shutter debugging, the unused camera’s TTL connection was found to hold the shutter open. Disconnecting that TTL input allowed the selected camera to control the shutter correctly.

Do not connect or disconnect detector or SDrive cables while the associated equipment is powered. Follow the laboratory hardware shutdown and startup procedure before changing cabling.

### SDrive checks

Before diagnosing the acquisition software, verify:

- the SDrive power indicator is on;
- the shutter override switch is in the `SHUT` position for automatic control;
- the selected detector TTL output is connected to SDrive `IN1` or `IN2`;
- the unused detector is not holding the other trigger input high;
- the SDrive shutter-output cable is connected to the iHR320;
- the physical shutter responds to the SDrive manual override.

The `OPEN` override position holds the shutter open regardless of the TTL inputs.

## Open- and closed-shutter acquisition

Signal frames are requested using:

```python
await spec.acquire_frame(
    open_shutter=True
)
```

Dark frames are requested using:

```python
await spec.acquire_frame(
    open_shutter=False
)
```

Internally, these calls use:

```python
await ccd.acquisition_start(
    open_shutter=open_shutter
)
```

For this hardware setup, the selected CCD controls the shutter by changing the detector TTL output connected to the SDrive.

Direct monochromator shutter commands should not be added around every exposure because the iHR is configured for external shutter control through the SDrive.

## Dark-subtraction modes

The range-acquisition workflow supports four dark modes.

### No subtraction

```python
dark_mode="none"
```

Acquires signal frames without acquiring or subtracting a dark.

### One dark per centre wavelength

```python
dark_mode="per_center"
```

Acquires one closed-shutter dark at each monochromator centre and reuses it for repeated signal frames at that centre.

### One dark per repeated frame

```python
dark_mode="per_frame"
```

Pairs each repeated signal acquisition with a closed-shutter acquisition before combining the corrected frames.

### Pre-taken stitched dark

```python
dark_mode="pre_taken"
```

Uses a previously acquired stitched `DarkSpectrum`.

This is intended for acquisitions such as hyperspectral mapping, where repeatedly moving the shutter and acquiring new dark frames would significantly increase acquisition time.

A reusable dark should only be reused when the relevant acquisition conditions remain suitable, including:

- detector
- wavelength range
- exposure
- gain
- speed
- ROI and binning
- grating
- slit width
- detector temperature
- spectrometer configuration

## Reusable dark spectra

A `DarkSpectrum` contains:

```text
wavelength_nm
intensity
```

Reusable darks can be:

- captured over a requested wavelength range;
- held in memory;
- saved as CSV;
- loaded from CSV;
- subtracted from later acquisitions.

Before using a reusable dark, verify that the SDrive shutter closes correctly and that no second detector is holding the shared shutter open.

## HORIBA diagnostic utilities

The HORIBA utilities are located in:

```text
scripts/horiba/
```

Run diagnostic scripts with the acquisition GUI and other HORIBA software closed, unless a script explicitly states otherwise.

### Check instrument configuration

```powershell
uv run python `
    .\scripts\horiba\check_horiba_configuration.py
```

This read-only tool reports:

- monochromator USB state
- busy state
- current wavelength
- grating turret position
- Slit A width
- exit-mirror position

### Set instrument configuration

```powershell
uv run python `
    .\scripts\horiba\set_horiba_configuration.py
```

Review the target values inside the script before running it.

The script sets and verifies:

- Slit A width
- exit-mirror position
- grating turret position

Changing the mirror or grating can change the active optical path or wavelength calibration.

### Test shutter behaviour

```powershell
uv run python `
    .\scripts\horiba\check_horiba_shutter.py
```

The shutter diagnostic acquires:

```text
Closed 1
Open 1
Closed 2
Open 2
Closed 3
Open 3
```

It displays:

- all six raw spectra;
- the mean open and mean closed spectra;
- the mean open-minus-closed spectrum;
- frame statistics;
- exact-equality checks.

The alternating order helps reveal source or detector drift.

If open and closed frames remain similarly illuminated, check the second SDrive trigger input before changing the subtraction code.

### Test Slit A response

```powershell
uv run python `
    .\scripts\horiba\check_horiba_slit_width.py
```

This diagnostic acquires spectra at several Slit A widths and plots the results together.

The original Slit A position should be restored before the script disconnects.

## USB and ICL behaviour

The HORIBA wrapper verifies that the monochromator reports an open USB connection after `mono.open()`.

Monochromator busy-state polling also checks the USB connection before each query.

If the connection is lost, polling stops rather than repeatedly issuing commands against a closed USB session.

If ICL repeatedly reports that the monochromator USB is not open:

1. close the GUI;
2. stop the Python process;
3. close other HORIBA applications;
4. confirm that no other process owns ICL;
5. check monochromator power and USB connection;
6. restart the hardware and ICL using the laboratory procedure;
7. run the read-only configuration check before acquiring.

Only one application should control ICL and the HORIBA devices at a time.

## ITC4000 laser control

The GUI optionally controls an ITC4000-compatible laser controller through PyVISA.

The implementation uses one persistent VISA session for:

- identification;
- current setpoint;
- current readback;
- laser-diode output;
- TEC output;
- controlled shutdown.

The software current limit is not a replacement for hardware limits, interlocks, operating procedures or optical-safety controls.

## Safety

This software controls laboratory hardware capable of moving optical components, energising shutters and enabling optical sources.

Before use:

- follow local laser and optical-safety procedures;
- verify the optical path before enabling a source;
- use suitable protective equipment;
- do not inspect an active optical port directly;
- do not disconnect powered detector or shutter-controller cables;
- do not exceed detector, shutter, laser or spectrometer operating limits;
- do not rely on software as the only safety mechanism;
- verify hardware readbacks after configuration changes.

Configuration-setting utilities intentionally retain the final verified hardware state.
